Chapter 1: Linear Algebra and Matrix Computation for Machine Learning

Introduction

Linear algebra is the language of modern machine learning. Models, inputs, weights, and operations are naturally represented as vectors and matrices, and understanding the algebraic operations and their... all affect performance. When arrays are strided (e.g., when taking subviews or transposes without copying), the stride value can cause inefficient memory access; some libraries will copy/transposes to a contiguous buffer before computation when that gives better performance.
------
On GPUs, memory coalescing plays a similar role. Threads are grouped into warps (e.g., 32 threads) and accesses by threads in a warp are fastest when consecutive threads access consecutive memory addresses. Blocked and tiled GPU kernels are designed... attention and key/query/value projections<|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|>
------
Multi-head attention extends single-head attention by computing multiple independent attention "heads" and concatenating their outputs. The motivation is to allow the model to attend to different types of information or different positions simultaneously. Formally, with $h$ heads, we..., or inference efficiency.

# Model scaling (width, depth, embedding size) and implications

Scaling a Transformer means increasing its capacity by making it wider, deeper, or increasing embedding sizes. Each axis of scaling has distinct computational and representational implications.
------
Increasing width: by increasing the model dimension $d$ (embedding size) or the intermediate FFN size $d_{ff}$, you allow each layer to represent richer features. Wider layers increase memory and per-layer compute roughly quadratically in some... the cost of token duplication, which must be considered during deduplication and effective token counting.

# Reporting metrics: accuracy, normalized scores, average across tasks
------
Choosing and reporting metrics requires care to fairly summarize performance across diverse tasks. The simplest task-level metric is accuracy: the fraction of examples for which the model's prediction equals the ground-truth label. Accuracy is appropriate for multiple-choice, yes/no, and... for typical values. We discuss three common heuristics for selecting $\alpha$: max-based scaling, mean-abs scaling, and RMS (standard deviation) scaling.<|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|>
------
Max-based scaling chooses $\alpha = \max_i |x_i|$ (or an empirical maximum plus small safety margin). This guarantees no clipping on the calibration set and is simple and robust. However, if the maximum is an outlier, the quantization... variance hurts convergence and final performance. Therefore quantization strategies frequently balance these competing effects: set a scale that ensures most values are represented without clipping (reducing bias) and use enough bits or fine-grained per-channel scales to keep variance small.
------
Analytically, when optimizing $\alpha$ to minimize MSE for a known distribution of $x$, one can write the MSE as a function of $\alpha$ and find its minimizer. # Definition of quantization and dequantization
------
At its core, quantization maps a real-valued scalar (or vector/matrix) into a discrete set of representable values. Suppose we have a real number $x \in \mathbb{R}$. # Normality assumptions and methods to test for normality<|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|>

Why consider normality? The normal distribution is analytically convenient: it is fully described by mean and variance, has well-known tail behavior, and many theoretical analyses (e.g., central limit arguments, propagation of variance through linear operations) rely on... allocating more representative levels to frequently occurring central values and fewer to extreme tails. Non-uniform quantization typically complicates hardware implementation; nevertheless, certain designs use piecewise-linear or logarithmic encodings to approximate non-uniform spacing while remaining implementable.
------

Conclusion
------
Robust evaluation of language models and chatbots requires a combination of standardized benchmarks, carefully designed human annotation protocols, awareness of dataset limitations, and rigorous statistical reporting. Benchmarks like GLUE and MMLU provide standardized tasks, while instruction and chat datasets... instruction–response pairs. Dialogue models must maintain context, track conversational state, and produce coherent turn‑taking behavior. Here are key considerations and best practices.<|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|>
------
Context windows and truncation. LLMs have finite context windows. For multi‑turn data, the combined length of system prompts, user turns, and model turns must fit within the model's maximum context size. Strategies include summarizing or compressing... strides aligning to bank count: e.g., declare shared arrays with a small extra column offset so that element addresses do not collide across threads.
- Use vectorized loads/stores when possible to reduce per-thread accesses and better align data across banks.
------
When using shared memory collaboratively (e.g., in tiled matmul), carefully selecting tile dimensions to align with bank counts and warp sizes is essential for avoiding conflicts and enabling efficient cooperative loads.

# Limitations of the analogy and when it does and does not apply

Analogies are useful but imperfect. Several limitations and caveats must be understood when relating Fourier phase to neural angular codes or semantic content more generally.
------
First, the Fourier basis is global and sinusoidal. Each Fourier coefficient influences the entire image (excepting special cases like localized transforms). Real neurons are localized in space and frequency, meaning cortical representations are more local and overcomplete relative to the global Fourier... precision causes instability.

Introduction

Probability and statistics provide the language and tools for formulating models of data, for evaluating how well those models fit observed data, and for optimizing model parameters so that the models are most consistent with observations. Many modern machine learning methods are built directly...(x;\theta)=\theta^\top x$ (linear logistic regression) then $f$ is linear and the log-likelihood is a concave function of $\theta$, which guarantees a unique global maximizer if the design matrix satisfies mild conditions.
------
The derivative of the logistic function is simple and crucial for gradient-based optimization:
\[
\sigma'(z)=\sigma(z)\big(1-\sigma(z)\big).
\]
This relation simplifies gradients because the derivative can be expressed in terms... by the parameter. When interpreting estimation results, always ask whether you require recovery of the exact parameter vector, or whether recovery of the predictive function suffices; your answers affect the appropriateness of MLE and the interpretation of uncertainty.

# Model scaling (width, depth, embedding size) and implications

Scaling a Transformer means increasing its capacity by making it wider, deeper, or increasing embedding sizes. Each axis of scaling has distinct computational and representational implications.
------
Increasing width: by increasing the model dimension $d$ (embedding size) or the intermediate FFN size $d_{ff}$, you allow each layer to represent richer features. Wider layers increase memory and per-layer compute roughly quadratically in some... the cost of token duplication, which must be considered during deduplication and effective token counting.

# Reporting metrics: accuracy, normalized scores, average across tasks
------
Choosing and reporting metrics requires care to fairly summarize performance across diverse tasks. We discuss three common heuristics for selecting $\alpha$: max-based scaling, mean-abs scaling, and RMS (standard deviation) scaling.<|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|>

Max-based scaling chooses $\alpha = \max_i |x_i|$ (or an empirical maximum plus small safety margin). This guarantees no clipping on the calibration set and is simple and robust. However, if the maximum is an outlier, the quantization... variance hurts convergence and final performance. Therefore quantization strategies frequently balance these competing effects: set a scale that ensures most values are represented without clipping (reducing bias) and use enough bits or fine-grained per-channel scales to keep variance small.
------

# Definition of quantization and dequantization
------
At its core, quantization maps a real-valued scalar (or vector/matrix) into a discrete set of representable values. Suppose we have a real number $x \in \mathbb{R}$. Robust evaluation of language models and chatbots requires a combination of standardized benchmarks, carefully designed human annotation protocols, awareness of dataset limitations, and rigorous statistical reporting. Dialogue models must maintain context, track conversational state, and produce coherent turn‑taking behavior. Here are key considerations and best practices.<|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|>
------
Context windows and truncation. LLMs have finite context windows. For multi‑turn data, the combined length of system prompts, user turns, and model turns must fit within the model's maximum context size. Strategies include summarizing or compressing... strides aligning to bank count: e.g., declare shared arrays with a small extra column offset so that element addresses do not collide across threads.
- Use vectorized loads/stores when possible to reduce per-thread accesses and better align data across banks.
------
When using shared memory collaboratively (e.g., in tiled matmul), carefully selecting tile dimensions to align with bank counts and warp sizes is essential for avoiding conflicts and enabling efficient cooperative loads.

# Limitations of the analogy and when it does and does not apply

Analogies are useful but imperfect. Several limitations and caveats must be understood when relating Fourier phase to neural angular codes or semantic content more generally.
------
First, the Fourier basis is global and sinusoidal. Each Fourier coefficient influences the entire image (excepting special cases like localized transforms). Real neurons are localized in space and frequency, meaning cortical representations are more local and overcomplete relative to the global Fourier... precision causes instability.

Introduction

Probability and statistics provide the language and tools for formulating models of data, for evaluating how well those models fit observed data, and for optimizing model parameters so that the models are most consistent with observations. Many modern machine learning methods are built directly...(x;\theta)=\theta^\top x$ (linear logistic regression) then $f$ is linear and the log-likelihood is a concave function of $\theta$, which guarantees a unique global maximizer if the design matrix satisfies mild conditions.
------
The derivative of the logistic function is simple and crucial for gradient-based optimization:
\[
\sigma'(z)=\sigma(z)\big(1-\sigma(z)\big).
\]
This relation simplifies gradients because the derivative can be expressed in terms... by the parameter. When interpreting estimation results, always ask whether you require recovery of the exact parameter vector, or whether recovery of the predictive function suffices; your answers affect the appropriateness of MLE and the interpretation of uncertainty.

Introduction

Models, inputs, weights, and operations are naturally represented as vectors and matrices, and understanding the algebraic operations and their... all affect performance. When arrays are strided (e.g., when taking subviews or transposes without copying), the stride value can cause inefficient memory access; some libraries will copy/transposes to a contiguous buffer before computation when that gives better performance.
------
On GPUs, memory coalescing plays a similar role. Threads are grouped into warps (e.g., 32 threads) and accesses by threads in a warp are fastest when consecutive threads access consecutive memory addresses. # Model scaling (width, depth, embedding size) and implications

Scaling a Transformer means increasing its capacity by making it wider, deeper, or increasing embedding sizes. Each axis of scaling has distinct computational and representational implications.
------
Increasing width: by increasing the model dimension $d$ (embedding size) or the intermediate FFN size $d_{ff}$, you allow each layer to represent richer features. Wider layers increase memory and per-layer compute roughly quadratically in some... the cost of token duplication, which must be considered during deduplication and effective token counting.

# Reporting metrics: accuracy, normalized scores, average across tasks
------
Choosing and reporting metrics requires care to fairly summarize performance across diverse tasks. The simplest task-level metric is accuracy: the fraction of examples for which the model's prediction equals the ground-truth label. Accuracy is appropriate for multiple-choice, yes/no, and... for typical values. We discuss three common heuristics for selecting $\alpha$: max-based scaling, mean-abs scaling, and RMS (standard deviation) scaling.<|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|>
------
Max-based scaling chooses $\alpha = \max_i |x_i|$ (or an empirical maximum plus small safety margin). This guarantees no clipping on the calibration set and is simple and robust. However, if the maximum is an outlier, the quantization... variance hurts convergence and final performance. Therefore quantization strategies frequently balance these competing effects: set a scale that ensures most values are represented without clipping (reducing bias) and use enough bits or fine-grained per-channel scales to keep variance small.
------
Analytically, when optimizing $\alpha$ to minimize MSE for a known distribution of $x$, one can write the MSE as a function of $\alpha$ and find its minimizer. # Definition of quantization and dequantization
------
At its core, quantization maps a real-valued scalar (or vector/matrix) into a discrete set of representable values. Suppose we have a real number $x \in \mathbb{R}$. # Normality assumptions and methods to test for normality<|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|>

Why consider normality? The normal distribution is analytically convenient: it is fully described by mean and variance, has well-known tail behavior, and many theoretical analyses (e.g., central limit arguments, propagation of variance through linear operations) rely on... allocating more representative levels to frequently occurring central values and fewer to extreme tails. Non-uniform quantization typically complicates hardware implementation; nevertheless, certain designs use piecewise-linear or logarithmic encodings to approximate non-uniform spacing while remaining implementable.
------

Conclusion
------
Robust evaluation of language models and chatbots requires a combination of standardized benchmarks, carefully designed human annotation protocols, awareness of dataset limitations, and rigorous statistical reporting. Benchmarks like GLUE and MMLU provide standardized tasks, while instruction and chat datasets... instruction–response pairs. Dialogue models must maintain context, track conversational state, and produce coherent turn‑taking behavior. Here are key considerations and best practices.<|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|>
------
Context windows and truncation. LLMs have finite context windows. For multi‑turn data, the combined length of system prompts, user turns, and model turns must fit within the model's maximum context size. Strategies include summarizing or compressing... strides aligning to bank count: e.g., declare shared arrays with a small extra column offset so that element addresses do not collide across threads.
- Use vectorized loads/stores when possible to reduce per-thread accesses and better align data across banks.
------
When using shared memory collaboratively (e.g., in tiled matmul), carefully selecting tile dimensions to align with bank counts and warp sizes is essential for avoiding conflicts and enabling efficient cooperative loads.

# Limitations of the analogy and when it does and does not apply

Analogies are useful but imperfect. Several limitations and caveats must be understood when relating Fourier phase to neural angular codes or semantic content more generally.
------
First, the Fourier basis is global and sinusoidal. Each Fourier coefficient influences the entire image (excepting special cases like localized transforms). Real neurons are localized in space and frequency, meaning cortical representations are more local and overcomplete relative to the global Fourier... precision causes instability.

Introduction

Probability and statistics provide the language and tools for formulating models of data, for evaluating how well those models fit observed data, and for optimizing model parameters so that the models are most consistent with observations. Many modern machine learning methods are built directly...(x;\theta)=\theta^\top x$ (linear logistic regression) then $f$ is linear and the log-likelihood is a concave function of $\theta$, which guarantees a unique global maximizer if the design matrix satisfies mild conditions.
------
The derivative of the logistic function is simple and crucial for gradient-based optimization:
\[
\sigma'(z)=\sigma(z)\big(1-\sigma(z)\big).
\]
This relation simplifies gradients because the derivative can be expressed in terms... by the parameter. When interpreting estimation results, always ask whether you require recovery of the exact parameter vector, or whether recovery of the predictive function suffices; your answers affect the appropriateness of MLE and the interpretation of uncertainty.

Chapter 1: Linear Algebra and Matrix Computation for Machine Learning

Introduction

Linear algebra is the language of modern machine learning. Models, inputs, weights, and operations are naturally represented as vectors and matrices, and understanding the algebraic operations and their... all affect performance. When arrays are strided (e.g., when taking subviews or transposes without copying), the stride value can cause inefficient memory access; some libraries will copy/transposes to a contiguous buffer before computation when that gives better performance.

# Model scaling (width, depth, embedding size) and implications

Scaling a Transformer means increasing its capacity by making it wider, deeper, or increasing embedding sizes. Each axis of scaling has distinct computational and representational implications.
------
Increasing width: by increasing the model dimension $d$ (embedding size) or the intermediate FFN size $d_{ff}$, you allow each layer to represent richer features. Wider layers increase memory and per-layer compute roughly quadratically in some... the cost of token duplication, which must be considered during deduplication and effective token counting.

# Reporting metrics: accuracy, normalized scores, average across tasks
------
Choosing and reporting metrics requires care to fairly summarize performance across diverse tasks. We discuss three common heuristics for selecting $\alpha$: max-based scaling, mean-abs scaling, and RMS (standard deviation) scaling.<|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|>

Max-based scaling chooses $\alpha = \max_i |x_i|$ (or an empirical maximum plus small safety margin). This guarantees no clipping on the calibration set and is simple and robust. However, if the maximum is an outlier, the quantization... variance hurts convergence and final performance. Therefore quantization strategies frequently balance these competing effects: set a scale that ensures most values are represented without clipping (reducing bias) and use enough bits or fine-grained per-channel scales to keep variance small.
------

# Definition of quantization and dequantization
------
At its core, quantization maps a real-valued scalar (or vector/matrix) into a discrete set of representable values. Suppose we have a real number $x \in \mathbb{R}$. Robust evaluation of language models and chatbots requires a combination of standardized benchmarks, carefully designed human annotation protocols, awareness of dataset limitations, and rigorous statistical reporting. Dialogue models must maintain context, track conversational state, and produce coherent turn‑taking behavior. Here are key considerations and best practices.<|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|><|pad|>
------
Context windows and truncation. LLMs have finite context windows. For multi‑turn data, the combined length of system prompts, user turns, and model turns must fit within the model's maximum context size. Strategies include summarizing or compressing... strides aligning to bank count: e.g., declare shared arrays with a small extra column offset so that element addresses do not collide across threads.
- Use vectorized loads/stores when possible to reduce per-thread accesses and better align data across banks.
------
When using shared memory collaboratively (e.g., in tiled matmul), carefully selecting tile dimensions to align with bank counts and warp sizes is essential for avoiding conflicts and enabling efficient cooperative loads.

# Limitations of the analogy and when it does and does not apply

Analogies are useful but imperfect. Several limitations and caveats must be understood when relating Fourier phase to neural angular codes or semantic content more generally.
------
First, the Fourier basis is global and sinusoidal. Each Fourier coefficient influences the entire image (excepting special cases like localized transforms). Real neurons are localized in space and frequency, meaning cortical representations are more local and overcomplete relative to the global Fourier... precision causes instability.

Introduction

Probability and statistics provide the language and tools for formulating models of data, for evaluating how well those models fit observed data, and for optimizing model parameters so that the models are most consistent with observations. Many modern machine learning methods are built directly...(x;\theta)=\theta^\top x$ (linear logistic regression) then $f$ is linear and the log-likelihood is a concave function of $\theta$, which guarantees a unique global maximizer if the design matrix satisfies mild conditions.
------
The derivative of the logistic function is simple and crucial for gradient-based optimization:
\[
\sigma'(z)=\sigma(z)\big(1-\sigma(z)\big).
\]
This relation simplifies gradients because the derivative can be expressed in terms... by the parameter. When interpreting estimation results, always ask whether you require recovery of the exact parameter vector, or whether recovery of the predictive function suffices; your answers affect the appropriateness of MLE and the interpretation of uncertainty.
